K2. **MERGED by the integrator, 11-15 19:54: 2c5765f on the main branch, pushed** (the three folders byte for byte your tested tree, so your runs were not repeated; on the main tree: whole build, gofmt, vet and the three packages' tests ok; your two reviews are `docs/review/core-watchdog-2026-11-15.md` and `core-watchdog-2-2026-11-15.md`; the roadmap's two rows; **`tgarchive-watchdog.exe` of 2c5765f is installed beside the collector since 19:54**, its help printed from another folder). **`wip/core-watchdog`** at e83b22c (pushed; copy `wt-core-watchdog`, clean; from d1af260; written by Core, session K, 11-15 19:43). **A crash log, a run history and a watchdog that restarts a collector that died: a separate small program, `tgarchive-watchdog`. Three NEW folders, no existing file edited, no `go.mod` change, no schema step: it meets nothing and can merge at any point in your order.**
    - **What:** `tgarchive-watchdog run -dir <folder> [-status-file <the collector's status file>] -- <program> [arguments]` starts the program (the installed collector with its `run ...` line) as a child on the same console, copies its two output streams to `collector.out.log` / `collector.err.log` in the folder (rotated at 16 MB), touches a marker every minute, and when the child ends puts one line into `history.jsonl` (300 kept) and, for a crash, the runtime's report into `crash-<time>.log` (10 kept). `history` prints the last 7 days; a `watchdog-status.json` is rewritten at every change and every minute for the pages. New: `internal/crashlog` (ported from the old app's `crashlog.go`), `internal/watchdog`, `cmd/tgarchive-watchdog`. Standard library only (a wall test in each). Briefs: `BRIEF-K2-watchdog.md`, `BRIEF-K2b-watchdog-fixes.md`, `BRIEF-K2c-watchdog-last-round.md`.
    - **When it restarts, and when never:** only on positive evidence that the child died by itself: the Go runtime's crash text in its error output with the process ended within 2 minutes of that text's first line, or a Windows exception exit code. **Never** after a refusal or a failed start (exit code 1 or 2 with the collector's own words), a clean stop, a stop that was asked (Ctrl-C or SIGTERM noted, held 20 minutes), a person's kill, the console's end. The delay doubles 2, 4, 8, 16, 30, 30 minutes (back to 2 after a run of 30 minutes); three deaths in a row each within 10 minutes of its start end it; at most 12 restarts in 24 hours; **never before the latest wait or lane gate in the collector's status file plus 60 s** (a second line of defence: the collector keeps its own waits). A watchdog that finds the last watchdog's child still alive, or a marker it cannot read, starts nothing.
    - **Privacy:** its own output, the history and its status file hold numbers, times, fixed words, and of a crash only what is proven to be the Go runtime's: a fatal message from a fixed list, a panic matching a fixed pattern of words and numbers, a function whose symbol starts with one of a fixed list of prefixes (the app's packages, Go, the libraries the app uses), a file name only under that function's own package folder. Never a panic's message, never a line of the child's output. The raw output logs and the crash report are the child's own text, kept locally as the start scripts keep them today. Known limit, said by the coder: a made-up line shaped exactly like `main.<word>(1)` above a real location line would still be quoted; the collector prints no such line.
    - **Review: one independent read-only review, two passes, probes by overlay** (`wt-core-watchdog-review-scratch/REVIEW.md` and `REVIEW-2.md`; copy both into `docs/review` at the merge, masking this PC's folder names as usual). First pass: 2 high (a line of the child could be quoted in the record; crash-looking text anywhere made any later non-zero end a crash), 3 medium, several low: all fixed in K2b but five left on purpose (the safe side each). Second pass: every one of those closed; new, low to medium: fixed in K2c (function prefixes; the child's end is its process's end and its streams are cut after 30 s; the stop's hold above the collector's 15 minutes; off Windows the living-child check by `/proc` and a process group of the child's own; the marker written by rename, fail closed). **Left on purpose, in the package's comment:** crash text that starts in the middle of a line the child had not ended (no restart: the safe side); a crash-looking line within 2 minutes before a real crash; the clock set back between two ends. No third pass: K2c's difference was read by me (the rewritten `watch`: the next child only after the exit AND after both copiers returned), and each item has a test that fails when it is undone.
    - **Full runs once, on e83b22c (`runs-k2/`, 19:41, the PC at 49% CPU and 4.1 GB free before):** whole Windows tests: `internal/crashlog` ok 4.2 s, `internal/watchdog` ok 2.0 s, `cmd/tgarchive-watchdog` ok 0.8 s; gofmt clean; `go vet` clean for Windows and for Linux; **the race detector on all three in Linux containers: ok (1.4 s, 6.5 s, 2.1 s), no data race** (the stream copiers, the keeper's lock and the wait for the exit are new concurrent code). The tests that differ by system (the process group, the living-child check through `/proc`, the lock between processes, a real child's crash text) were seen PASS by name in a Linux container, not skipped.
    - **Not done, and whose:** it has never supervised a real collector (the first use is the day-long run, started by the owner: `PLAN-CORE-real-days.md`); the stale status file is only counted, nothing is killed (a hang is not handled); no page shows its status yet (pages). **To use it:** build `tgarchive-watchdog.exe` beside the installed collector (yours, with the installs); the `a3` start scripts then start the collector through it (mine, once it is installed); the app's starter `app\tgarchive.ps1` likewise (pages: message below). The collector needs no change.
    - **Roadmap rows:** "Crash log, run history" from "part" to "fake" (built, tested; no real crash met); a new row "a watchdog restarts a collector that died" (fake). The old "A failing task restarts alone" is unchanged (inside the collector).

   head fa8c011 is merged in. `internal/catalog` only; no schema step; no concurrent code.** Independent of C8.
   Brief `BRIEF-E12-set-check-first.md`; report `archiver/docs/progress/copies-setmark-REPORT.md` on the branch.
   - **What it changes.** `exactcheck.go`: `markCheckFirst(ctx, id, collected)` takes the caller's collected
     condition and files held for the topic's or the chat's preference; new `markCandidatesSQL`,
     `markCheckFirstIn` (one read over the set for the files with rung 6's edge, each decided by the existing
     per-file rule). `ladder.go`: `wantedAsMainIn` marks its set first; `wantedAsMainSQL` has FOUR statements
     now: the first two share one clause (wanted, or held as a probable copy by its edge: **a file held for the
     chat's preference that has a probable edge is no longer made plain wanted**; a stale mark is cleared when
     a file becomes plain wanted), the third no longer takes a fetch-real file, the fourth makes a held
     look-alike the owner released or set to fetch for real wanted and unmarked. `atsave.go`: **the step at
     save also records rung 6's edge for a covered video held as not collected** (chat, kind, topic,
     alternative; its row untouched; a count `lookalike.edged_at_save`): that is what makes a video in a chat
     chosen LATER held or marked, not plain wanted (P3). `topics.go`: comments only. Tests: `setmark_test.go`,
     `setmark_review_test.go`, `setmark_cost_test.go`, `atsave_p3_test.go`, and Core's scenario test as
     `chosen_p3_test.go` (its four subtests pass); one line in `topics_size_test.go` (the new read's plan).
   - **One line in another track's file, after the merge of fa8c011:** `chosen.go` `judgeChat`'s call became
     `c.markCheckFirst(ctx, id, collected)` (commit 0bd9533: the function gained that argument; the condition
     is the one `judgeChat`'s own read asks; behaviour unchanged). That loop of `judgeChat` is now partly
     redundant (`wantedAsMainIn` marks the set itself): Core's to remove (board message 45).
   - **The rule about "different", for the record:** a file is marked only while a saved file of its byte size
     remains that it has not been compared with (the per-pair exclusion of `exactCandidatesSQL`, as on the
     base). A file-wide guard my brief asked for was removed again in the fix round: it kept a file from being
     compared with a NEW saved file of its size.

     No hard rule broken, no defect introduced; its findings fixed (the guard above; two holes older than this

     whole run of the catalogue on 0bd9533 FAILED two of this piece's own tests** (`runs-copies-e12/`): their
     expectations met the main head's `topicHoldSQL` (`released = 0`: a released file is never held for its

   - **Cost, said plainly.** A topic action on a busy topic (2,000 files each in 100 messages) is 20 to 30%
     slower as a whole (2.6 to 3.4 s before, 3.0 to 4.3 s after, CPU 28 to 72% from other work); the longest
     single write under the one writer did not grow beyond noise (46 to 64 ms against 49 to 67 ms). A save
     that covers held-not-collected files costs about 0.7 ms a file more (75, 87, 145 ms for a save covering
     100 queued look-alikes plus 0, 10, 100 such files); the step's median is unchanged (351 microseconds a
     saved video).
   - **Known, not fixed.** A file whose check was undecided is checked again after each owner action on its
     topic or chat (one check per action; as on the base). An edge outlives the saved file it points at: a
     file whose only saved look-alike is lost is still held as a probable copy when collected (the old app
     released such holds; it belongs with confirm or release). A covered file that is set aside at the save
     gets no edge (documented in `atsave.go`). `TestTopicSettleAtSize` still judges real seconds (pages').

Read by me: the difference since c2f7232 outside the merge is that one line, the test file and the report section; the logs' counts (0 "DATA RACE", 0 "FAIL" in the six). **Not run by me: the whole `internal/collector`, on Windows or under the detector, on this head** (the line changes one answer of the fake that no other test asked for; your next whole run of the collector covers it). **Known, left, read only:** the fake pages topics by "ids above this one" and ignores the limit, which fits the product's reader and is not Telegram's order; no other answer of the fake was found with a required field left empty (every object it builds was read).
    - As first written (K-2, 21:11): **The fake Telegram no longer hands one object to two encodes: the data race of board message 52 (a) and 53 (`runs-pages-g1/race-collector.log`). Test scaffolding only: five files in `internal/collector/faketg` and the coder's report; no product code, no schema step, no `go.mod` change. It holds no merge and can merge at any point.**
    - **The race:** `invoker.Invoke` encodes its answer, and the library's `Encode` writes the encoded object's flags. A channel's stored event message is put into every difference answer that reaches it (two accounts on one channel, or one account's update managers across a restart: the failing `TestTelegramHoursAcrossRestart`), so two answers encoded one object at once. A second way, confirmed: the update pushed to the event's first account was that same object, and the product's `RecordOf` reads it and encodes it.
    - **The fix (`server.go` 65 lines, `conn.go` 4, `join.go` 2, `remote.go` 2):** `Server.encode` encodes every answer under a lock of its own (never held with the server's or the world's lock, never across a call out; the decode is outside it); `Server.deliver` hands every pushed update to the handler as a copy encoded and decoded into fresh objects (both push sites: `pushOne`, `Join`). Three more unlocked reads of the fake's own data closed by reading, not seen in a log and not covered by the new test: a message's fields in `msgObj` and `history` (copied under the world's lock), a channel's pts in `refreshChannel`.
    - **Brief and report:** `BRIEF-K7-faketg-race.md`; `archiver/docs/progress/core-faketg-REPORT.md` on the branch (every encode and push site of the package and what was done with each).
    - **Read by Core, the whole difference of the four source files:** the new lock is taken alone; `msgObj` now takes the world's lock for a snapshot, and none of its six callers holds that lock or the server's at the call (`history`, `refreshChannel`, `refreshCommon`, two in `getChannelDifference`, `pushOne`). No independent review: a lock and a copy in a test fake.
    - **The named test, `TestAnswersSharingADocumentEncodeApart` (`share_race_test.go`, 200 lines, no sleep, no real-time bound; the coder's runs):** on the package before the fix, under the detector: 18 race reports (encode against encode on the message and its media, getter against encode) and FAIL; after it: three runs in a row of the whole `faketg` package under the detector (`race-archiver.sh`, 2 CPUs, 3 GB), 0 reports, ok each (1.3 to 1.4 s).
    - **Full runs, once, on c2f7232 (`runs-k7/`):** the whole `internal/collector` on Windows (the only package that uses the fake, 26 files): ok, 558 s; gofmt clean. **No whole race run of the collector by me** (82 minutes): the fake's own package had its three, and your next whole race run of the collector covers the rest; say here if you want it from me before the merge.
    - **One visible change in the rig, for whoever reads a test later:** a pushed update is now decoded from bytes, so its flags are set as off the wire. Before, an object the fake had never encoded carried none, and the library's getters then answered "none" (a live-pushed forward had no origin unless something had encoded the object first). The collector's whole tests pass with it. Empty lists in a pushed update are now nil, not empty.
    - **Not done, known:** the loopback path (`remote.go` `serveOne`) got the same lock and is not exercised by the new test (it needs a listener); the speed of the collector's big tests with every encode in turn was not measured apart (the whole package took 558 s here against 681 s in the pages track's run on another tree and load: no sign of a slowdown).

A check's frames (`ffmpeg.Config.SampleHWAccel`): a frame whose card process fails is read once more on the CPU and that read is the frame's; a card process that hangs switches the runner's sampling to the CPU at once and its time is given back to the call once (at most one process limit); one blame a call. **A decoder is a speed choice, never a verdict:** statuses, categories, outcomes, missing parts and the resume are untouched.     - **Against the main branch, eight files:** `internal/watchdog/status.go` (+86: the write by a temporary file and a rename that is tried again within a budget), `loop.go` (+36: the budget per write, the marker's write retried, one output line when the last write is lost), new `rename_windows.go` (the POSIX-semantics rename, `os.Root.Rename`) and `rename_other.go` (the old rename elsewhere), new `readstatus.go` (**the readers' door: `watchdog.ReadStatus(dir) (StatusDoc, error)` and `ErrStatusNotWhole`**), two test files, the coder's report `archiver/docs/progress/core-wdrename-REPORT.md` (no path of this PC in it; it has a section "For readers of the status file").

A real `Get-Content -Raw` loop: old 2,032 and 1,569 of 3,000, new 6, 6 and 3. Every read was a whole document.
    - **What it does:** on Windows the rename is the one Windows carries out while a reader has the file open with delete sharing; only a refusal a reader can cause (access denied, a sharing violation) is tried again, with pauses doubling from 10 ms, for 200 ms in all on a routine write and 2 s on the last write at the watchdog's end (a lost last write left "running" on disk); a write finally lost is counted as before (`status_write_failures`), removes its temporary file and leaves the earlier document; one new count `renames_retried`; every existing field and name of the document kept. The marker `running` is written by `internal/crashlog` with the old rename: the watchdog tries a refused marker write again for 200 ms; the history file is left as it is (a retry could write a line twice).
    - **What no writer can give, confirmed by the review's probe, and why the door exists:** a reader can get "file not found" on the name for an instant while the writer is inside the rename call itself, under the old rename and the new one alike (it is Windows' own rename over an existing file; the toolchain's rename never deletes the name first; after each call the name was there 48,000 of 48,000 times); and a reader without delete sharing is refused right after each rename that went through (3,008 to 4,533 failed opens per 3,000 writes with the new rename, 1,036 with the old). **So every reader reads again:** `ReadStatus` opens with delete sharing and reads again within about 100 ms on "not found", a refused open or a body that does not parse. **For pages:** the launcher (`internal/launcher/status.go`, `findLeft`) reads the file once: use the door (board message 92). **Core's own:** the `a3` scripts read it with `Get-Content`: they get the same read-again after this is merged.

   - **What it does.**
     - The catalogue: `AddOwnerMirrorWant` (one transaction; refusals write nothing; an owner's stop is cleared by a
       new want); the word's doors `SetMirrorWord`, `MirrorWordOf`, `MirrorWords`, `CarryMirrorWord`;
       `MirrorChatRows` (every chat with a want, an open mirror, a mark or a word, in 9 statements whatever the number
       of chats: 0.4 s at 300 mirrors and 200,100 forward rows by the coder, 274 ms with 8 statements by the review's
       first pass); `MirrorCreatesSince`; `MirrorEndStopped` as a valid end word.
     - The doors in `internal/mirror` (`door.go`): `States`, `PagePlan` and `Plan.Token`, `Want`, `Never`, `Allow`,
       `Stop`, `SettleWord`, `BlockOffers`, `CheckNotMade`; catalogue only; every per-account door refuses an account
       not on record as signed in (`ErrNotSignedIn`) and, at the collector, an account the collector does not have.
     - The task's changes (`auto.go`, `auto_word.go`): the owner's wants first, each oldest first; a create takes the
       want's starter; the cap of new mirrors per day counts every starter; `OwnerOnly` keeps only the owner's wants in
       a run without `-auto-mirror`; the words are read once a turn; `settleWord` carries an owner's word out with
       one `ReadMirror` call; `createWord` handles a lost create.
     - The collector's part (`mirror_door.go`, `mirror_auto.go`, one place in `command.go`): the task turns for a
       signed-in account without the switch, and then reads the wants first and returns unless an owner's want is
       live (no request, no line); `MirrorWant` makes the forward secret's file when absent; after a write the
       task is nudged (`turnDue`, then `nudgeReader()`); the doors listed below.
     - The settle's word: the owner's word names the block it was chosen for (`ChatState.Block`, a SHA-256 token, 24
       hex); `SettleWord` refuses with `ErrBlockChanged`, nothing written, when the block moved; `BlockOffers` is the
       one map of which words a block offers, used by `States`, by the door and by the task.
     - The stop: `Stop` and `Never` write nothing to Telegram; no request's intent is written after the mark or the stop
       is on record (the intent row is the boundary; see N1 below); an allow does not undo the owner's stop.
     - The intent check inside the intent's own transaction: `intentHeld`, called by `BeginMirrorCreate(By)` and
       `BeginMirrorBatch`: the want neither ended nor blocked, no never mark on the chat's family, the chat not
       protected, and (M9e) the open mirror row's own end word. Held: nothing written, `*IntentHeldError`
       (`ErrIntentHeld`); the task ends the turn with no request, the command ends `NotSent` in the plan's words.

   - **What it does.** A fourth seam `server.Staging` (nil in `tgarchive app`: the page then shows the copy queue from the catalogue and says the rest needs the program). **The verdict**, in fixed words, the state that needs the owner first: the library's disk full; the library away since a time; the staging disk away (with the time of that mark, and "readable now" when the page's own read succeeds); no room; copying (N of M busy); files waiting with no copier busy, split by cause (due now; failed and tried again at a time; put off while a check reads it; not published yet; held by the wanting-again cap); waiting for a read-back in speed-limited hours; nothing to copy. After "library away" or "full" the sentence's second half comes from staging's own state at the same reading: downloads go on into staging only when it has room, and are said to stop when it has none. **The numbers**, each with what it counts and its reading's time: free space and what is kept free, waiting, copied and not verified, failing now, failed before, stuck, written straight to the library this hour; "copied and verified: X MB/s over the last 5 minutes; it moves when a file ends" (never 0.0 while copiers are busy). **What needs you:** each stuck file with its reason, what one click costs and "Try its copy again" (ONE statement by key on the ledger; refused with 409 when it is no longer stuck; not offered where a retry cannot help: a malformed hash in the catalogue's record); the wanting-again hold with "Let it go on (up to N downloads again this hour)", refused with 409 unless the hold is on at that moment; staging without room and the library away for 10 minutes; the library's disk full. The Overview carries the count with a link. `/api/live` carries the store's gauges and reads no table; the queue is read again by its floor only (5 s; 30 s after a reading over 250 ms).
   - **Review.** One independent, read-only, with probes: `REVIEW-G5-staging.md` (2 must fix, 7 should, 14 minor), every finding fixed in the fix round with a named test, four mutations each failing its test; the two must-fixes: the verdict promised downloads into staging when staging had no room; a release with nothing held reached the store and reset the hour's count. **The review's table: un-sticking is safe for every reason a file is stuck** (it never publishes bad bytes, never replaces a library file, never loops; its cost can be one full copy over the link, and the button says so). The fix round had no second pass (it did what was decided; its departures are in the report: the stuck table is keyed by the store's stop reason; a third clause when staging's free space is not read yet; the unstick route reads the row before its one write). Seen in a browser by its coder at desktop and phone width, twice.

One reading costs 139 to 202 ms at 100,000 ledger rows on a busy PC (more than half of it the store's own queue summary, read through the gauges): near the 250 ms line above which the page reads only every 30 s. With small files the verdict can switch between "copying" and "waiting (due now)" from one reading to the next (both true at their moment).

The client's acting side: a click whose outcome is "not known" is settled from what the catalogue already holds of the chat. IT SENDS NOTHING: no request of any kind is added. Go code only in `catalog/schema_act.go` (the schema text of step 21 untouched); no schema step; no goroutine. Never run against Telegram.**
    - **Its own difference on top of K9b (11 files changed, 3395 insertions(+), 14 deletions(-)):** new `internal/catalog/act_settle.go` (the match per kind and the doors `SettleClicks`, `ClickCandidates`, `SettleRepeat`, `ClickNotArrived`) with four test files; Go code in `internal/catalog/schema_act.go` (reason words; `SendClickAgain` for the chat-refusal word only, a second door `SendClickAgainNotInChat` for the owner's "it is not in the chat"); `internal/collector/click.go` (the settle called from the clicks' step in the reader's goroutine, with its pace; the check before a repeat goes out) and `click_api.go` (the entry points below); `click_settle_test.go` and small changes in the older click tests; the coder's report `archiver/docs/progress/core-settle-REPORT.md` (sections 10 to 13 are the three fix rounds and the merge; section 11 has the match as one table; no path of this PC in it).
    - **What it does.** When a send, a reply, a forward, an edit or a delete ends "not known" (the answer was lost: it may or may not have been carried out), the app looks at what the live monitor stored of that chat. **The app marks the click "arrived" by itself only when the match is exact and alone; otherwise it only shows the candidates and the owner decides.** A "send again" is ended "not sent: the earlier try is in the chat" before it goes out when its first try is found the same way. A reaction and a read mark are never settled by the app (the catalogue holds nothing that tells).
    - **The match, as built after three review looks:** a send's or reply's message is a plain text message of the account's own in that chat with exactly the click's text (byte for byte as stored), the same reply target and topic, **not forwarded, no media, never edited** (no earlier version and no edit date), dated from 10 s before the click was marked "sending" to 5 minutes after it (the 10 s only for the offset between this PC's clock and Telegram's), each try judged on its own window, and not tied to another click; a forward is matched per message by the post its copy names (so only where the copy names the source post: a broadcast channel's own post, or a source that is itself a forward; a forward from a private chat, a basic group or a supergroup always waits for the owner) and its topic; an edit by the message's current text with a version after the mark; a delete by the deleted mark (never for a "delete for everyone" in a private chat or basic group: the mark cannot say for whom). **The app gives no verdict of its own when:** more than one candidate fits; it cannot tell whose the message is (a broadcast channel's post); another click of the account with the same content, or any forward click into that chat, lies within one window's length on either side; the account already sent the same text in that chat in the 7 days before (a text the owner repeats: one indexed read, at most 5,000 messages, reaching the cap counts as repeated); the chat's last 7 days are not all on record (its history read is not done, or the live monitor has an open gap for the chat or, for a private chat or basic group, the common box); the owner said "it is not in the chat" for that click.
    - **The app's verdict is never the last word.** An "arrived" the app made keeps its text (until the 7-day rule) and can be taken back by the owner ("it is not in the chat": back to "not known" for every "not known" row of the click; the rejected message stays on record and is never a candidate for any click again; the app does not settle that click again). A repeat the app stopped can be sent again on the owner's explicit word, which is a different word from "try again" (the chat's remembered refusal) and does not lift it. An "arrived" by the owner's own verdict is final as before.
    - **For the pages track (piece 3: the routes) and the viewer (piece 4), the collector's entry points this adds in `click_api.go`:** the candidates of a click to read (each with the fixed word why the app would not take it); `ClickNotArrived(ctx, ref)`; and **`SendClickAgain(ctx, ref, tryAgain, notInChat)` now takes two words** (it had one on K9b's branch). A row the app settled carries the fixed word "found in the chat by the app", so a page can tell it from the owner's own verdict.
    - **Briefs:** `BRIEF-K9c-click-settle.md`, `BRIEF-K9c2-fixes.md`. One sentence of my first brief was [name removed] and cost a must-fix (it led to one window over a whole chain of tries).
    - **Independent review, three looks, read-only, 14 probes on catalogue rows written by the real writers: `REVIEW-K9c-settle.md` in the project folder** (for `docs/review`: its header names this PC's folders; replace them). First (cee2e466): "may be listed after the must-fixes": 2 must fix shown by probes (the app said "arrived" for clicks that were not carried out: the window was the hull of a whole chain of tries; a forwarded message was taken for a send's message), 3 should fix, 6 minor. Second (78b9c76c): eight closed; one new must-fix (the owner's "it is not in the chat" was overridden through the chain's other tries) and four should-fixes. Third (97866e9f): **"MAY BE LISTED"**; three small should-fixes (the rejected message could be taken again; an open gap did not block; one old stored message made "on record" pass), fixed in 6dea8c26 with the reviewer's probes as tests, each seen failing first; no fourth look. Then K9b's final head merged in by the coder with no conflict (d6415255). Found sound throughout: it sends nothing; every writing statement names the state it leaves and the chain's tip; each check runs in its own transaction; the text match is byte for byte; privacy.

    - **What stays, said plainly for the owner:** (1) **the design's accepted risk:** the click did not go out, and the owner sent the identical plain text, once, by hand from another device within the 5 minutes after it, in a chat whose last 7 days are on record and hold no earlier sending of that text: the app says "arrived" (or stops a repeat); the owner can take it back. The same for a post forwarded by hand inside a forward click's window. (2) a PC clock more than about 5 minutes ahead of Telegram's can make an earlier click take a later click's message. (3) a text with a link is mostly not found by the app (a preview counts as media, a preview filled in later as an edit): the careful side, the owner decides. (4) the guard's word is read just before the transaction: a message stored in the milliseconds between is not seen that time. (5) "Look in the chat" (one read request, its cost said) is not built: a later piece.

   **Two things in one branch, both in `tgarchive-window`: (1) THE GATE: in BOTH modes the browser engine can ask nothing but the named pages on 127.0.0.1; (2) THE PAGES MODE: `tgarchive-window -pages ... -data ...` shows the app's own pages in a window and nothing of the viewer (the owner, 07:40: "Will it eventually be standalone (i.e. not in a browser)"; the launcher's side is the pages track's G11).** No schema step, no `go.mod` change (`golang.org/x/sys/windows` was already required), nothing against Telegram; only `internal/window` and `cmd/tgarchive-window` (31 files, 6,377 insertions, 71 deletions against 6af0c09a).
   - **Why the gate, and why it changes the viewer's window too.** The independent security review of the pages mode measured that the WebView2 engine sends requests of its own at every start, **in the viewer's window as merged on 11-15 as well**: its component updater and its configuration service at Microsoft, with DNS lookups; and that a shown page's sub-resources go anywhere and a refused navigation has sent its request before the window refuses it. The window runs outside the VPN. Nothing of the archive or of a page is in the engine's own requests, and the review found no way from anything in the archive to a request; what leaves is the PC's own address. **The gate (`gate.go`, `engine.go`):** a listener of the window's own process on `127.0.0.1:0` that the engine is started with as its only proxy (loopback not bypassed, no host name ever resolved, the component updater and background networking off; the engine's `WEBVIEW2_*` variables removed at the start and the arguments variable removed again once the engine has answered, so no child program inherits it). It forwards a request only when it is plain `http` to an absolute address whose host is exactly an allowed `127.0.0.1:PORT` (the pages mode: the named ports; the viewer's mode: the viewer's own port, set once); everything else is answered 403 and counted, `CONNECT` included (so every https and every WebSocket, also to an allowed port). Its own outgoing side dials `tcp4` to an allowed address only. A fixed one-line script added to every document removes the WebRTC and WebTransport constructors (the only thing that still sent packets past a proxy). The engine's crash reporting is switched to "custom" through an environment options object of the window's own (`options_windows.go`), so that Windows does not send the engine's crash data. Autofill, password saving and the reputation check are set off and read back, in both modes.
   - **The pages mode, for G11 (the full contract is in board messages 111 a, 113 c and 116 c; unchanged since).** `tgarchive-window.exe -pages http://127.0.0.1:PORT/ [up to four] -data FULL-PATH`; every viewer flag refused with it; port 80 refused. Exit 0: the person closed the window, or a pages window on that folder was open and was brought forward; exit 3: no window can be shown (no runtime or one older than 120, the gate or the engine not started, not within 8 s, nothing shown within 10 s by a watch outside the message loop, the engine ended for good); exit 1: flags and other errors. Only the named addresses are shown; a new window to a named address opens in the same window; a link to the open web shows the window's own line with the address and "Copy" (nothing is handed to Windows); a `text/plain` download from a named address is saved in Downloads under a name of the window's own, every other download refused; a named page that answers 500 or more with words of its own is shown as it is, an empty one, a server that is gone and 15 s of opening show the window's line with "Try again"; the user agent ends in ` tgarchive-window`. A mutex, a window class and an engine folder of its own (`window-pages-webview` in `-data`), so it can run beside a viewer's window. Without `-pages` the program's flags and words are what they were.
   - **Reviews.** One independent, read-only, security-minded, with probes and the engine's own net log: `wt-b6-review-W-scratch/REVIEW.md`: **"not as it stands"**, 11 findings (the engine's traffic; sub-resources; the two modes sharing one instance and engine folder; autofill keeping a form's value; the exit bound; an empty 500; port 80; inherited engine variables; crash dumps).    - **The setting `pages_open` in `tgarchive.json`:** the app's own window (the default, also when the key is absent) or the browser; a choice on the setup page, and a small form "Where the app's pages open" on the launcher's running and failure pages (stored first, taking effect at once in both directions; it writes only that key). The pages' port is named at the setup when nothing answers on it then, so the first start keeps one window; the viewer's address is never named.

   - **Whose window it is:** the person closing the window changes nothing and nothing restarts it; a failure after that is shown by starting the window again on the launcher's page, once per event. **A second start of `tgarchive.exe` opens no window:** it sends one `POST /show-window` (a header of its own; refused for anything a browser adds, another Host, another method) to the running launcher and ends; the launcher then, at most once in 2 s, brings its window to the front by one start of the window program, starts a closed one again, or (browser setting) opens its page as before. The launcher ends its own window at its own end (a clean stop through its Ctrl-C helper, then by the handle after 3 s), when the pages end for good (the window is restarted on the launcher's page alone), and when the pages' address changes (restarted on the new one). **The window, and only it, is in a kill-on-close job of the launcher's** (opened by its number, taken only when its creation time matches the one held since its start): a launcher that dies takes its window with it; the watchdog, the collector and the viewer are outside it and run on as today.
   - **The bound on starts of the window program:** one per start of the launcher; one more for each event that replaces its own window (the pages ended for good, a start on changed addresses, a failure after the person's close, a change of the choice to the window, a press of "Try the window again"); one front start per ask, at most one in 2 s. Never per refresh, never per look of the launcher's watch (`TestWindowNeverLoops`: a window that always exits 0 at once: 1 start in the run).

No schema step, no `go.mod` change, nothing against Telegram.
   **The switch "mirror chats I join by themselves" is stored and set from a page; an account added or signed in while the program runs needs no restart; the chat list's interval while it is on is a setting (the owner, 11-16: "default to 10, with some fuzziness"); a switch-on asks for one chat list at once (the owner, 11-16: "Yes").** Briefs `BRIEF-M10-auto-switch.md` (with its "what the old app already knows" note), `BRIEF-M10b-fixes.md`, `BRIEF-M10c-list-every.md`, `BRIEF-M10d-list-at-once.md`, `BRIEF-M10e-last.md`; the coder's report `archiver/docs/progress/mirror-autonew-REPORT.md`; the review `archiver/docs/review/mirror-autonew-2026-11-16.md` (on the branch).
   - **What it does.** One settings row per account, `mirror.auto.new.N` (`on <time>` or `off <time>`; absent: off; anything else: "cannot be read", never on and never empty). The task reads it at every turn before it decides anything, so nothing automatic is sent in a turn that read it off or unreadable; the start reads it for the accounts of the start, before any task. On and not set up: the set-up the start used to do once (`autoSetUp`: the forward secret, "since", the baseline, the policy for new chats, ready). Off: no longer set up, the policy removed, and the reads of the chats the task took earlier are ended too (the mirror's end of a read, M11's field; cleared at the next set-up). Unreadable: as off, the policy left. `run -auto-mirror` stays and means "on for this run": the stored row is not read, and the door refuses.
   - **"On" means from now on (rule 1 of TRIED-BEFORE 24).** A page's "on" that was written sets "since" to the later of now and the baseline on record plus one, in the row's transaction; a baseline older than "since" is no baseline (`autoBaseline`, which every reader of the baseline goes through; the row cannot be deleted inside a transaction, the catalogue has no such delete). So a chat joined while the switch was off is never taken by a later "on"; a second "on" moves nothing. A run with the flag after a stored "off" (or an unreadable row) moves "since" the same way, at most once a run per account, and says so; the flag's behaviour between two flag runs is as before (chats joined in between are taken).
   - **The chat list.** The interval while the automatic mirror is on is one catalogue setting, `mirror.auto.list_every` (minutes; default 10, range 5 to 1440), with a random part on top of each interval (0 to a fifth of it, at most 5 minutes, never shorter than the set value, the same after a restart, none with `-auto-mirror-steady` or without a secret); a value that cannot be read: 30 minutes, said once a session. `Config.AutoListEvery`: 0 (now the default: its `def` line is out of `collector.go`) means the stored setting; any other value is used exactly (the tests' override). A written "on" from the page asks for exactly one chat list at once (a one-shot mark on the account, taken in `chatListEvery`; `reader.go` is not touched): a list that fails, meets a wait or sets no baseline brings no second one before the interval; an "off" clears a mark not yet used.
   - **The doors for pages (catalogue only; no request but the one list above):** `SetMirrorAutoNew(ctx, account int64, on bool) error` (refused, nothing written: an unknown account; for "on" an account not signed in on record; a run with the flag: `ErrAutoNewByRun`; an "off" is accepted whatever the sign-in says); `MirrorTask` keeps `AutoJoined` (set up and running now) and `AutoNewSettable` (now true unless the run has the flag) and gains `AutoNew` (the switch in force), `AutoNewWhy`, `AutoNewUnreadable`, `AutoNewFrom` (chats joined after it are taken; zero: none yet); `MirrorListEvery(ctx) (MirrorListEveryFacts{Minutes, Stored, Default, Unreadable, SpreadMaxSeconds}, error)`, `SetMirrorListEvery(ctx, minutes int) error`. `Plan.Token()` now binds the account and the chat (pages treats it as opaque). Pages' lent lines (`MirrorWantRead`, `wantApprove`) are taken as they are; their comments say so.

K16. **MERGED by the integrator of 06:55, 11-16 14:15: 3eb9b11 on the main branch, pushed (the commit before K14's e9b8c54)** (`git diff f7d6ca63 baa84077` on 2b55760, clean; tested on the joined tree with K14: the runs of K14's line). **`wip/core-batch`** at **baa84077** (pushed to `private`; copy `wt-core-batch`, clean; cut from f7d6ca63; a trial merge with the main head 2b557601 is clean (`git merge-tree`, 13:02); written 13:02 on 11-16 by Core, session K-4). **READY. A panic inside a catalogue write no longer leaves the one writer held (the copies track's board messages 113 a and 116).**
    - **Against the main branch (3 files):** `internal/catalog/catalog.go` (+34 -2), a new `batch_panic_test.go` (6 tests), the coder's report `archiver/docs/progress/core-batch-REPORT.md`. No schema step; nothing outside `internal/catalog`; no test removed.
    - **What was wrong:** `Batch` rolled back only on a returned error. A panic in the caller's function left the transaction open on the single writing connection; `Batch`'s own deferred `preparePending` then waited on that connection for good where a statement was pending, and where none was, whoever recovered the panic (the collector's supervisor recovers every task) went on with a writer every later write waited on. Nothing crashed, nothing moved.
    - **The fix:** a second defer in `Batch`, registered after `preparePending`'s so that it runs first: when the function did not return (a panic or `runtime.Goexit`) the transaction is rolled back, then the pending statements are prepared on the free connection, then the panic goes on with its own value (nothing recovers or wraps it). A returned error and a commit are as before. **The schema steps at `Open` (`migrate`) had the same hole** (a step that panicked left the file's write lock held: a later `Open` failed busy) and got one deferred rollback. Every other writer of the package goes through `Batch`. Noted, not changed: `checkpoint.go`'s `once` holds the writer's connection across its work with a close that is not deferred, in a goroutine with no recover (a panic there ends the program): if a recover is ever added there, that close must become a defer first.
    - **Brief:** `BRIEF-K16-batch-panic.md`. **Review:** no separate independent review: the fix is the one two independent reviewers of the copies track specified (`REVIEW-E19-owner-verdicts.md` finding 5, `REVIEW-E18b-second-look.md` finding 1) and both their probes pass on it unchanged; the 34 lines read by K-4.
    - **Tests:** all six new tests failed by their own timers on the old code and pass on the new (the coder's report). **Runs, once, on baa84077 (`runs-k16/win-baa84077.log`; one script): build, gofmt, vet exit 0; the whole `internal/catalog` ok, 484.1 s.** No race run: the change starts no goroutine.
    - **NOT verified.** A panic inside `Commit` or the driver's own rollback; the collector's supervisor end to end (only the catalogue's half is tested).
    - **For copies after the merge:** the judge's exception (a panic from inside a catalogue call raised again instead of going on) can go, in a later piece of theirs.

But the reader as merged with C13 reads the file in place, read-only, while the viewer has it open, and then: (a) SQLite sets its own reader marks in the viewer's `-shm` file at every such read (the file and its `-wal` stay byte for byte: the piece's test compares them and holds every other byte of `-shm` equal); (b) when the viewer closes between the reader's look and its open, that open makes `-wal` and `-shm` anew in the viewer's folder and they stay until the viewer's next open (the reviewer's probe; rare; no data in them). `readonly_shm=1` does not stop (a) on this build (probed). **Fixed in words in this piece** (the help, the flag's usage, the start's line and the comments said "never written to, made": they now say exactly the above); a reader that touches nothing (private copies only, the viewer's `-wal` included) is a second way of reading with its own review, and is built only if the owner says "copies only" to item 38 (recommended: keep as built).

   - **What it does.** `act_door.go` is the one file of the viewer that connects: to exactly `127.0.0.1:<port>` of the door file, with the door's secret in its one header, one connection a call, no proxy, no redirect, no retry of any changing call ever; "nothing left this program" is kept apart from "it left and no answer came". The two commands hand it a finder over `actdoor.ReadDoorFile` on the folder of the very catalogue they opened (the file's name looked for first: the old archive has no door file ever, and then nothing is drawn and nothing is asked, as before). **The lent lines:** the door file also names the catalogue file it serves (its base name), and the finder takes a door only when it is its own catalogue's: before, a second catalogue file in the same folder (a copy, another archive) took the other one's door, and a click could have gone to another chat for real. **One click, one request:** the page's key goes to the door unchanged on every try; the door's key memory (K14) answers a known key with its click; **the viewer believes a click's number only when the catalogue's key row (`catalog.ActKey`) names that very click**, so nothing but the collector can make the page say "queued"; a key the catalogue already knows goes straight to the door past the viewer's own gates, so a retry is never refused by the first try's own cap or wait; the state read learns of a queued click by its key, also with the collector away or the viewer started again. The door's state is used for: the largest text; a caption's limit, but only where the message is surely a caption (it is part of an album: the catalogue records a link preview's picture exactly as a sent one), a too-long caption of a single photo being left to Telegram's own refusal; a wait, a slow mode or the cap running for the kind shown before the click, by the collector's own clock.

No schema step; no `go.mod` change (the driver is the one already used). It is on the path of every statement the catalogue runs.
   - **What was wrong (shown by the copies track's reviewer with probes: board message 151, `REVIEW-E26-judge-recovers-doors.md` finding 1):** `database/sql` gives a connection back after a driver's panic only on its plain exec path. A Go panic inside the SQLite driver in a cached write or any read inside `Catalog.Batch` made K16's deferred rollback wait for good: the goroutine hung holding the one writer, no panic came up, no supervisor was reached, every write of the program waited and the program did not end; a panic in `Commit` or a cached write outside a transaction kept the writer; each panic in a read kept one of the 8 reader connections. The watchdog does nothing about a hang. No such panic is on record; its cost would be a silent stall for good.
   - **The decision (mine; the reviewer of copies had proposed "recover, mark the connection broken, go on"): the process ends.** After a Go panic inside the translated C code SQLite's own state in the process (its mutexes, the per-file objects every connection shares) cannot be trusted, and closing a "broken" connection calls into the same library. SQLite's file is safe against the process ending at any instant; the watchdog keeps the report as a crash and starts the program again by its own policy; the start recovers as after any kill.
   - **How:** a new package `internal/sqlguard` (it imports no package of ours): one registered driver, `sqlguard.DriverName` ("sqlite-guarded"), that is the very driver value registered as "sqlite" behind wrapper values. Every method that enters the inner driver (29, on the driver, a connection, a statement, a transaction and rows) defers one function that, when a panic is on its way up, writes one report to the standard error output and exits with code 2 (the runtime's own code), never returning into `database/sql`. The report: a line that starts "panic: ", this guard's fixed words, the panic value's Go TYPE, its text only for the runtime's own errors, then the goroutine's stack from the panic's frame with every argument list written "(...)". The write is on a goroutine of its own and the exit comes after it or after 5 s, whichever is first (an error output that is a full pipe nobody reads must not hold the end). Each wrapper value implements exactly the optional `database/sql/driver` interfaces its inner value implements (checked at run time: a value with another set is refused with an error, never wrapped half-way). **The catalogue's three opens name it (`catalog.go` two, `checkpoint.go` one); nothing else in the catalogue changes: not `Batch`, not K16's rollback (it stays for a panic of the catalogue's own code).**
   - **Files (against 78739d59):** new `internal/sqlguard` (`sqlguard.go`, `wrap.go`, `interfaces.go`, six test files); `internal/catalog/catalog.go` +8 −4 and `checkpoint.go` +4 −2 (the driver's name); `internal/watchdog/` one new test file (the guard's real child classed by the watchdog's own reader and keeper); **ONE LENT LINE in the viewer track's `internal/viewer/wall_test.go` (its own commit, 2f45d61a: `internal/sqlguard` is an allowed dependency of the viewer through the catalogue; it imports no package of ours and no Telegram library): viewer, yours to take or word otherwise**; the report `archiver/docs/progress/core-guard-REPORT.md`. Test functions removed: 0.

   - **The cost, measured twice, plain against guarded in one process, three rounds each:** `ClaimNext` at 200,000 rows 1.24 to 1.52 ms against 1.27 to 1.69 (the coder: 1.81 to 2.01 against 1.79 to 1.90); a cached write in a Batch 12 to 31 µs against 13 to 41; a prepared read 5.1 to 5.8 µs against 5.0 to 5.3; allocations per call 602, 32, 20 against 610, 33, 21.      app, ... the mirror function would automatically begin, go back to the beginning of that group ... and

   - **What it adds.** `run -auto-mirror` (**off by default**, one constant; with it `-auto-mirror-download`,
     `-auto-mirror-all-messages`, `-auto-mirror-new-per-day` 5, the pause and per-hour values). With it on: the
     chat list is asked every 30 minutes (topic lists keep their own interval); after each stored list a look
     takes chats joined after the baseline (the first whole list after the switch was first on; groups,
     supergroups and channels only; never a marked one, a mirror, one that ever had a mirror); the reader's own
     loop reads such a chat (an approval marked as the mirror's); **one new step in the reader's turn, at most
     one writing request per turn for the account** (a create or one batch), with not-before times instead of
     sleeps; new media kept current; "never mirror" marks (`mirror -never N`, `-allow N`, `-list`; written only
     with the collector stopped); a refused create or batch blocks, nothing loops; an error on a forward blocks,
     never ends a mirror for good; a lost create's channel is adopted from the chat list by its name.
     **Settling:** a batch left sending, unknown or "already" is settled from what the mirror chat holds, by
     exact evidence only (a forward's own origin; else the same Telegram media; only the settle's own finished
     read begun after the request plus 60 s; only mirror messages above what the ledger knew); one message that
     cannot be told leaves the batch as it is; `mirror -settle N` and `-take arrived|not-arrived` (the owner's
     word), `-create not-made`; at most 3 settles of a mirror a day by itself. The pause's start is 20 s now.
   - **Files outside the mirror's own.** `internal/collector/reader.go`: `ReadApproval` gains `By`;
     `EstimateRead` keeps a mirror's approval; `ApproveRead` makes it the owner's and switches the chat on; the
     idle wakes at the turn's not-before time; the chat-list interval is a function; `autoListed` after a stored
     list; `autoTurn` between gaps and the history pass; the topic lists' own due time. `account.go`: three
     fields and the monitor's sink through `autoSink` (the store itself with the switch off).
     `internal/catalog/rows.go` `EnsureChat`: **with a stored policy (set only by `-auto-mirror` without its

         tries once more); a person's open goes before the thumbnail makers' (bounded at 30 s); each open is
         timed in three steps, with slow opens banded by seconds since start and whose they were, in the
         counts-only snapshot. **The stall's cause is still not known: the next slow start will name the step.**

18. **`wip/b-window`** at 65d20f2 (pushed; written by B4, the viewer session, 11-15 19:26). **The native window with
    mpv** (the owner, 11-15 12:40: "yes, plan the native window with mpv"; and 15:49 on converted videos: "very
    slow ... so seeking is impossible"). **It holds the main head fa8c011** (merged in as 276e167 before the
    full runs; entry 17 is in main as 0697f15, byte for byte this branch's viewer base). Apply
    `git diff fa8c011 65d20f2`: 44 files.
    - **What is new:** the package `archiver/internal/window` and the command `archiver/cmd/tgarchive-window`
      (Windows; elsewhere it builds as a stub that says so). **What changes in existing code:**
      `archiver/internal/viewer` (`play.go`, `launch*.go`, `stats.go`, `viewer.go`, `web/js/play.js`,
      `item.js`, `help`/`settings` lines, tests, the wall test's one line for `unsafe` in
      `launch_windows.go`) and nothing else. No schema, no catalogue, no route, no access class.
    - **`go.mod`, as you asked in message 28:** one new requirement, pinned:
      `github.com/jchv/go-webview2 v0.0.0-20260205173254-56598839c808` (MIT; pure Go, no cgo), used only by
      `internal/window`, and through it `github.com/jchv/go-winloader v0.0.0-20250406163304-c1995be93bd1`
      (ISC; indirect). `go.sum` gains six lines (the two modules' `h1` and `go.mod` lines, and the `go.mod`
      lines of two old `golang.org/x/sys` versions those modules name; the app's own `golang.org/x/sys`
      still wins). **Why not our own COM layer on `golang.org/x/sys/windows`:** the window now does create
      the WebView2 itself with its own handlers (the binding's `Embed` could end the program or wait for
      ever); what it still takes from the module is the loader (`webviewloader`, which loads Microsoft's
      `WebView2Loader.dll` from memory, embedded in the module) and a settings type. Writing the loader
      ourselves would mean shipping that DLL by another road. The whole module adds about 0.5 MB.
    - **What it is:** one program, `tgarchive-window.exe`, that runs the same viewer in its own process on a
      free 127.0.0.1 port and shows its pages in a WebView2 window; a saved video plays in mpv (a child
      process drawing into the window, kept warm, driven over an already connected inherited pipe) as the
      file is: no converting, free seeking. In a plain browser and on a phone nothing changes (the pages
      talk to the shell only where it exists). mpv is not in the repository: the program finds it by
      `-mpv`, `MPV_PATH`, beside itself, or on PATH read by hand; without it, or without WebView2, the
      browser's own path is used.
    - **What bounds it (each tested or shown by the self-check):** a page message is one of ten exact shapes
      with bounds, or dropped and counted; a file is named only by its numeric id, turned into a path by
      the viewer's own rule (`viewer.PathOf`, one function with "Open in the player", no route) and handed
      to mpv over the pipe, never on a command line; mpv's command line is 52 fixed options (no config, no
      key bindings but a click's pause, no scripts but its controller, no network, the lavf demuxer only
      with a format and a protocol whitelist, no references followed, a cap on a picture's size, bounded
      caches); a watchdog kills and restarts an mpv that stops answering; navigation elsewhere, new
      windows, permissions and downloads other than the viewer's own file route are refused; the DLL
      search is limited to system folders; the program sits in a kill-on-close job (mpv and the WebView2
      die with it; the player of "Open in the player" and the browser fallback are let go on purpose).
      **Also for the browser viewer:** "Open in the player" refuses a program under the Windows folder and
      hands a named player only files with a media extension (a finding of this branch's review that
      applied to the merged viewer).
    - **Reviewed: two independent read-only looks, security-minded** (`REVIEW-B4-window.md`: 3 confirmed, 7
      plausible, among them downloads not refused and the job killing the owner's player;
      `REVIEW-B4-window-2.md` after the WebView2's creation was rewritten: the earlier findings gone, every
      hand-written COM slot checked against the binding's source and generated bindings, one confirmed new
      defect that mattered: the picture cap made mpv retry its decoders without end, now bounded by the
      watchdog). **Every finding fixed** but N10, accepted. **Read by me:** the message parser's rules, the

   - **What it does.** `mirror -adopt N -into M` (N the source's chat number in the catalogue, M the existing chat's; `-keep-current`, off by default; media only by default or every message, as the mirror command). Never done by anything automatic. The plan is read-only and refuses in fixed words (N has a mirror or a want; M is one of the app's mirrors, a mirror row's chat, a source, or N, or the migration twin of N; M or its twin taken; not a channel or supergroup; a "never" mark on either; N protected; the account not in either, or frozen); it says that whether the account may post in M is not on record and the first forward shows it, and that what the owner forwards into M by hand from now on is not known to the app. Then three questions: both chats' sizes; the reads (each chat by a pass this command began and that finished); the counts ("A are in the chat, B are not, C cannot be told; B will be forwarded in about K batches; M holds U messages that are not N's") and the record, after which the usual run of the mirror command forwards B. `mirror -adopt N -take arrived|not-arrived` is the owner's word for the C later (refused while a batch of the mirror blocks).
   - **The weigh (`internal/catalog/mirroradopt.go`): the settle's rules in set form over a whole chat, never by text, date or position.** By the post a copy names when that key is no other message's of the source (more than one copy: there, counted twice); a key several source messages share: "not there" only when nothing in the chat names it or shares a media row; by media only with exactly one bare candidate, the key unique in the source and every media row exclusive on both sides; "not there" only when no message of the chat, whatever its origin, shares any media row; everything else "cannot tell". 0.33 to 0.44 s at 50,000 by 50,000 invented messages. **A copy told "there" is confirmed to exist now when the chat had been read before this command** (`internal/telegram/mirror_ids.go`, new: `channels.getMessages`, 100 ids a request, under the refresh's wait key and counted under its kind; its cost shown with the reads; a copy that is gone is marked deleted exactly as a live delete marks it, and the weigh is done again; an answer that does not name every id, a wait over the bound or any failure: nothing is recorded).
   - **The record (`schema_mirroradopt.go`), one transaction, everything checked again inside it** (the refusals, both reads, no open row, the weigh done again and compared by a digest of every verdict): the mirror row (created, M its chat, started by the owner, `adopted_at`, `adopt_top`, `adopt_all`, `adopt_src_top`, its stored name M's title: catalogue only; base one blank and the source's chat number as ordinal: no number of the app's names); the "there" messages as forward rows on one batch row "done" whose reason says no request went out; the "cannot tell" messages in `c_mirror_adopt_wait`. **Never written: `chat.mirror_of`, M's download switch, a `c_chat_made` row, a name by the name rule** (every reader of `mirror_of` means "the app's own chat": the owner's switch and the client's writes go on working on M). After an adoption that weighed media only, a message without media up to `adopt_src_top` is never a candidate, whatever a later run or the automatic task asks for (it was not weighed); the plans say so. `-keep-current` writes the owner's want through the page's own function; without it the automatic task sends nothing for N or M. A forward refused for the chat's posting rights (CHAT_WRITE_FORBIDDEN, CHAT_ADMIN_REQUIRED, CHAT_RESTRICTED, CHAT_GUEST_SEND_FORBIDDEN, every CHAT_SEND_..._FORBIDDEN) blocks that mirror in the want's existing words: one forward request in all over eight turns of the automatic task. The settle on an adopted mirror looks only above the larger of the ledger's top and `adopt_top`, and takes a message of the chat that shares no media row with the batch as "not the batch's"; **on an app-made mirror every settle result is as before** (the reviewer: 0 of 300 random worlds differ; the existing settle tests unchanged). No schema step; no `go.mod` change. **It makes the program send history requests for one chat without a click: never run against Telegram.**
   - **What it does, and no more:** after an account's chat list is stored (`reader.go` `readTurn`, one call, after the mirror's), for an account that is signed in, not frozen and has no mark yet: when the stored switch is on and readable, the account's own chat is stored, **that chat is switched off for downloads**, and NO read record of any kind exists for it, ONE read approval is written with a word of its own (`By: "self_chat"`) together with a durable mark for the account, in one transaction. A chat that has any record gets the mark alone and its record is left byte for byte. Once the mark exists the step never writes again for that account. It sends no request itself, asks no estimate, and does not choose the chat for downloads. The reader then reads that chat as it reads any approved chat (its pace, its counts, every wait slept in full), and the live monitor keeps it current.
   - **What it never does (each a test):** no other chat gets a history request; no file of the own chat is wanted, supplied or downloaded (the chat stays switched off; **under `-new-chats-download`, where the chat's row is made collecting, the step does nothing and says one line: the review's probe showed 49 files downloaded with no click before this**); nothing is written over an estimate, the owner's approval, the mirror's approval, a mirror's end or a record that cannot be read; an owner who later says yes makes the record the owner's.
   - **The switch:** one stored setting per catalogue, `reader.self_chat`, on when absent. Off, or unreadable (fail closed, one fixed line): the step writes nothing, AND the reader does not read the own chat unless its record is the owner's own approval (`readChats` sets a flag once per reader's turn; honoured in `readTurn`'s topics loop and turn loop and in `readGap`'s common-box loop; the chat stays in the list; the record and the cursor stay, so "on" again reads on from where it stopped). There is no owner's door that ends a read in the app today; this switch is the owner's way to stop this one.
   - **Doors.** The viewer: `(*catalog.Catalog).SelfChat(ctx, account) (chat int64, ok bool, err error)`, on a read-only handle (the mark the viewer asked for: a read of what is stored, `account.tg_user_id` against the account's own space; no column added).
